name: writing-style
description: Apply Marin house style when producing or revising commit, pull-request, issue, comment, documentation, report, or blog prose.
---

# Marin House Style

Read the medium-specific guide for the deliverable, then apply
[ai-writing-donts.md](ai-writing-donts.md) as the final prose pass.

## Read The Right File

- Read [blog-posts.md](blog-posts.md) for public explainers and blog posts.
- Read [reports.md](reports.md) for technical reports aimed at peer researchers outside Marin.
- Read [tutorials.md](tutorials.md) for learning-oriented documentation that introduces Marin.
- Read [reference-docs.md](reference-docs.md) for precise usage docs aimed at readers who already know Marin.
- Read [issues.md](issues.md) for standard OSS issues and experiment issues.
- Read [pull-requests.md](pull-requests.md) for commit messages and PR titles and bodies.
- Read [discord.md](discord.md) for Discord summaries and tactical replies.
- If a piece spans media, use the stricter applicable rule.

## Voice

Write as a rigorous open-science lab building foundation models. Treat
experiments, decisions, and mistakes as part of the record. Use a sober,
practical voice for technically capable peers. Be natural and direct; use
specialized language when it improves precision.

## On Voice

These rules are stricter for agents. Preserve harmless human flourishes when
editing another person's prose.

## Evidence and scope

- Lead with the result, decision, or changed behavior.
- Support claims with numbers, examples, observations, and relevant artifacts.
- State uncertainty and limitations plainly; do not imply broader evidence.
- Remove hype, product-announcement language, AGI speculation, decorative
  emoji, and adjectives standing in for evidence.
- Explain non-standard terms. Add detail only when it helps the reader
  reproduce, evaluate, or act.

## Add Structure For Longer-Form Writing

Long-form pieces may need a document-level TL;DR and section takeaways. Do not
force them into short media.

Before publishing, confirm the prose is factually current, sounds normal when
spoken to a colleague, cites material evidence, and contains no generic filler.

# Reports

Use this file for technical reports and research writeups.

## Assume This Reader

- Assume the reader is a peer researcher working in the same general area.
- Do not assume the reader uses Marin or cares about Marin as a codebase.
- Assume the reader cares about the result, the experimental setup, and the high-level methodology.
- Do not assume familiarity with your specific method or terminology.

## Optimize For

- Give enough detail for someone to reproduce, critique, or extend the work in their own stack.
- Make the main result and methodological choice legible without forcing the reader through Marin-specific context.
- Preserve first-read clarity instead of chasing exhaustive completeness.

## Write This Way

- Lead with the result, then show how you got there, then state caveats.
- Include a doc-level TL;DR near the top.
- Add section-level takeaway lines when sections are long enough that readers need orientation.
- Define non-standard or ambiguous terms briefly before relying on them.
- Include concrete setup details, metrics, and comparison points.
- Describe methodology at the level needed for a peer to evaluate the work, not at the level of Marin implementation internals unless those internals matter to the result.
- Remove repo-local detail that does not help an outside researcher interpret the result.
- Link to repo internals when they help with reproduction or provide useful context (similar to an appendix in a paper).
- Clearly separate confirmed results from exploratory findings (e.g., label as exploratory, replicated, or stable).
- Cite the prior work that the report builds on or compares against, including relevant Marin experiments and external papers where appropriate.

## Test

- Ask whether a researcher in another codebase or organization could evaluate the work without outside context.
# AI-Writing Don'ts

Use this as the final prose pass for agent-written Marin text. Delete first.
Rewrite only when the sentence carries a fact, decision, result, constraint, or
caveat.

## Use Plain Technical English

Apply Orwell's rules of writing and Simplified Technical English (ASD-STE100).
Prefer short, active sentences, common concrete words, one action or claim per
sentence, and one term for each concept. Cut words that add no fact. Avoid stale
metaphors and idioms. Keep specialized terms when they improve precision, and
explain unfamiliar terms. If a term's origin or exact meaning is unclear, omit
it or research it until you can explain it to other readers. Repeating jargon
solely because another source used it hides uncertainty and spreads undefined
language. Break a rule when following it would make the prose less clear or
accurate.

## Test With A Cold Reader

For durable prose or any text that must stand alone, give a context-less
subagent only the finished draft and its intended audience. Do not provide the
conversation, source notes, diff, or author intent. Ask what terms, referents,
claims, or causal links a first-time reader cannot recover from the text.
Fix concrete gaps with the smallest local edit. Extra context can hide the main
claim and overload a first-time reader. Narrow or reshape a claim when its full
explanation would interrupt the text; put optional background behind a link. Do
not add conversation history that the intended audience does not need, and do
not turn personal style preferences into findings.

## Cut Empty Sentences

Delete a sentence when removing it loses no information. Common waste:

- bridge prose: `The main change is...`, `Stepping back...`, `What this means is...`
- importance claims: `Importantly`, `It is worth noting`, `This is the key point`
- stage directions: `Let's dive in`, `Here is a summary`, `In this section...`
- effort narration: `After extensive investigation...`
- generic conclusions, recaps, future-work sections, and chatbot sign-offs
- prose that restates a heading, list length, table, diff, or visible structure

Start with the fact. End when the useful information ends.

## Ban Stock Contrast

Remove rhetorical templates built around a staged opposition:

- `X, not Y`
- `not X, but Y`
- `not just X, but also Y`
- `more than X`
- `the question is no longer X; it is Y`
- `the real story is X`
- `what matters is X`
- `rather than Y` when no real comparison is being made

State X directly. Split real comparisons into plain claims.

Bad:

> The issue is not query execution, but the metadata path.

Better:

> Metadata loading takes 12 seconds. Query execution takes 400 ms.

Bad:

> The change improves reliability, not just performance.

Better:

> Median latency fell 18%. Failed retries fell from 7 to 0.

Grammatical negation is fine when the negative fact matters: `The ablation has
not run.` A measured comparison is fine when both sides matter.

## Require Evidence

- Name the code path, configuration, issue, artifact, measurement, or unknown.
- Replace adjectives with evidence. Delete `robust`, `powerful`, `seamless`,
  `significant`, `crucial`, and `pivotal` unless a concrete result follows.
- Say `we have not tested X` when evidence is missing.
- Keep prose consistent with tables, code, and current behavior.
- Remove stale explanations after the implementation changes.
- Use one precise term for one technical object.

## Avoid LLM Cadence

- Skip adjective trios and forced rules of three.
- Skip `Additionally`, `Furthermore`, `Moreover`, `Importantly`, and `Notably`.
- Skip `landscape`, `ecosystem`, `interplay`, `tapestry`, `testament`, `delve`,
  `showcase`, `underscore`, and `foster`.
- Avoid em-dash asides. Make the point a sentence or delete it.
- Avoid bold labels, takeaway boxes, and decorative headings.
- Do not rotate synonyms for the same object.

## Score Every Paragraph

Each paragraph must contain a concrete result, setup, decision, constraint, or
caveat. Rewrite or delete paragraphs that only frame significance or smooth a
transition.

Ask of every sentence:

1. What information disappears if I delete it?
2. Can I replace it with a number, named fact, decision, or explicit unknown?
3. Does it still describe the current code and evidence?
4. Could it appear unchanged in a random software announcement?

Delete sentences that fail this check.

## Prefer Plain Rewrites

- `The main change is that retries are bounded.` → `Retries stop after five attempts.`
- `This highlights a scalability problem.` → `Registration takes 1.2 seconds for 100 workers.`
- `The current state is much clearer.` → Name the resolved and open questions.
- `This is procedural progress.` → `The code is ready. The ablation has not run.`
- `The configuration has three parts:` → Start the list.

Finish with a search for stock contrast, bridge phrases, unsupported adjectives,
and stale claims. Prefer a shorter accurate document.

   102	## H6 arm spec (iteration 1 candidate, pending its own review)
   103	
   104	Treatment: `ARM_XLA_FLAGS="--xla_gpu_memory_limit_slop_factor=90"`, everything
   105	else identical to control; env-only (no commit), recorded in arms.tsv extra
   106	column. Mechanism: raises the scheduler/remat temp-arena cap from 113.6 GiB
   107	(85) to ~120.2 GiB (90, at the documented pool-free-space bound); the 85
   108	calibration predates the carry offload (peak now ~116.5 GiB, was ~138) and
   109	train.py's own comment says the smaller arena makes HloRematerialization
   110	recompute more of the step.
   111	
   112	Draws: one T at slop90; if delta > bar candidate, a second T draw; controls
   113	bracket from iteration-0 draws plus a fresh control after (the keep rule's
   114	>=2C/>=2T). Ladder: OOM at 90 -> 88; green+gain at 90 -> consider 92 with the
   115	explicit caveat that arena 122.9 GiB exceeds the documented 120.2 bound and
   116	must survive a fresh-mapping against ~17 GiB physical outside the pool.
   117	
   118	Engagement check: memory/peak_gib (scored as peak_gib_max) must RISE vs
   119	control. MFU moved but peak did not -> not engaged, inspect task-log XLA_FLAGS
   120	echo before concluding anything. Failure modes: named first-step NCCL alloc
   121	abort or BFC OOM (cheap crash, ladder down).
   122	
   123	Confounds pre-declared: (a) remat disengaging when peak crosses the allocator
   124	boundary is the DESIRED mechanism here, not an artifact (#8054's trap inverted)
   125	— but drops and the paired loss series must stay clean; (b) cuda_async
   126	MEM_FRACTION is a RELEASE threshold (#8490): if the higher peak crosses it,
   127	the pool unmaps every step and the arm gets slower with high step-time
   128	variance — watch mfu_stdev, and treat a high-variance slowdown as
   129	threshold-crossing, not as "slop raise is bad".
     1	# Campaign: ragged EP hero MFU loop (loop-260828-8753mfu)
     2	
     3	Goal: raise hero MFU beyond the #8753 stack's frontier without fidelity loss
     4	(no added quantization; loss trajectory equivalent or better) and without
     5	breaking hero-checkpoint compatibility (every keep must hot-swap into the live
     6	hero run; checkpoint migration OK, restore from hero checkpoints must work).
     7	
     8	Cost model (user, 2026-08-29): rack capacity is use-it-or-lose-it — failed or
     9	extra arms are cheap (stay courteous to other rack users). The expensive
    10	failure is a WRONG CONCLUSION: being sent in a bad direction, or prematurely
    11	closing a promising one, because a flawed experiment misled the loop. Review
    12	cycles and arm design optimize for inferential validity first, rack frugality
    13	second. Concretely: every treatment arm pre-declares an ENGAGEMENT CHECK
    14	(evidence the lever actually engaged — a kernel name in a profile, an echoed
    15	flag, an HLO property, or a metric that cannot move unless the lever did);
    16	"no effect" results are only recorded as dead after engagement is confirmed;
    17	near-threshold results get more draws rather than a coin-flip call.
    18	
    19	## Base
    20	
    21	- Branch `research/mcwitt/8753-mfu-loop` @ 04026e94fb = PR #8753 head
    22	  (`mcwitt/8317-hero-step-optims`, based on xla-fork/#8684).
    23	- Contents: offload_carry remat + latency-hiding scheduler (forced
    24	  overlap-limit 1), bitcast pair-interleave, FA4 128x64 wide forward tile as
    25	  `gpu_fa4_cute_wide` (landed by implementation name; dispatch has passed unit
    26	  tests but has NOT yet run on a rack — the iteration-0 control validates it).
    27	- PJRT wheel: `jax_cuda13_pjrt-0.11.1+marin.c9526e8c0272` pinned in
    28	  pyproject/uv.lock (GitHub release, marin-xla-pjrt-20260828). No wheel
    29	  overlay in arms.
    30	- Reference numbers: 24k-restore same-night pair (2026-08-28): control (#8753
    31	  stack sans tile) 23.06/23.05, +tile 23.32/23.37 MFU. 30k-restore cross-ref:
    32	  plain-#8684 gate 22.31 median. Protocols are not comparable; this campaign
    33	  re-baselines at 30k.
    34	
    35	## Fixed protocol (every arm)
    36	
    37	- One NVL72 rack, cw-us-east-08a, `experiments.grug.moe_hero_ep.launch_diagnostics`,
    38	  one arm on the rack at a time.
    39	- Restore from the latest hero permanent checkpoint
    40	  `s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin/grug/hero-12d8b6f0-dee637/2026.08.19.2/checkpoints/step-30000`
    41	  (verified complete: manifest.json present). `--num-steps 30030` (ABSOLUTE
    42	  stop step: checkpoint step + 30; `--num-steps 30` exits vacuously right
    43	  after restore).
    44	- Mixture data (trained-router routing), cf 1.15, `ragged_all_to_all`,
    45	  `--master-params device` (this branch renamed the master-less mode
    46	  `disabled` -> `device`; in-process migration reads a master-bearing
    47	  checkpoint's fp32 master directly into device params), schedule-steps
    48	  4470000, no checkpoints/evals. Profiler off on scored arms EXCEPT an
    49	  allowed profile tail: `PROFILE_STEPS` few, `PROFILE_START_STEP` ABSOLUTE
    50	  (e.g. 30021) so it lands past the scoring window — profiler start_step is
    51	  a global step; a relative value like 5 silently never fires on a restore.
    52	- `JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR` rotated per run id (clique-init deadlock dodge)
    53	  → every arm cold-compiles ~9.5 min; pre-phase (env sync + restore +
    54	  migration) ~19 min cold, less when the object cache is warm.
    55	- Priority: production allowed (user 2026-08-28) with job runtime capped
    56	  under 1 hour: iris `--timeout 3500`. Watchdog: COMPILE_CEILING=1800 from
    57	  first W&B-run existence, STEP_BUDGET=1200 (20-min post-compile cap, user),
    58	  early release at +19 steps, cancel on preemption/failure/coord death;
    59	  QUEUE_CEILING bounds pending time (queueing doesn't count against the 1h).
    60	- SERIALIZATION: one arm on the rack at a time, and arm N+1 is submitted only
    61	  after arm N's watchdog exits — the coordinator's iris `--timeout 3500`
    62	  clock starts when the coordinator starts (it schedules instantly and waits
    63	  on its child), so an arm queued behind another arm burns its 1h budget in
    64	  the queue and gets killed just as its train job starts.
    65	- RESUBMISSION: an invalid arm (watchdog kill, preemption, incomplete
    66	  window) is resubmitted with a FRESH RID and VERSION, never reused — a
    67	  reused RID merges W&B histories (silent window mixing), reuses the
    68	  leader-populated compile cache (the documented clique-init deadlock
    69	  recipe), and can vacuously reuse the artifact layer.
    70	- Hero liveness: the live hero is NOT currently training (step-30000 is the
    71	  newest permanent checkpoint; no hero job in iris). Before each arm, check
    72	  the rack queue; if the hero ever reappears on this cluster, pause the
    73	  campaign and reassess priorities rather than contending with it.
    74	- Rack etiquette: queue arms back-to-back while work is pending; do not
    75	  submit anything if the loop is blocked >1h on analysis.
    76	- Timestamp bookkeeping (calibration): for each arm record W&B-run-created
    77	  -> first-step wall time. The compile ceiling starts at run creation, which
    78	  is BEFORE restore + master migration + compile; a ceiling kill on a cold
    79	  arm is not evidence against the arm until these timings say the ceiling
    80	  actually fits the work.
    81	
    82	## Metric and guards
    83	
    84	- Metric: mean `throughput/mfu` over scored steps +5..+19 relative to the
    85	  first logged step (`score.py --relative`). Higher is better.
    86	- Guards (from the same run): `moe/drop_fraction` max/mean not worse than the
    87	  paired control; `train/loss` at the window's last step equivalent to
    88	  control (bf16-noise band; 24k arms showed 1.29964-65 across four arms);
    89	  `window_complete` must be true.
    90	- Noise model: only ONE NVL72 rack exists, so there is no placement variance
    91	  (user-supplied fact, 2026-08-29; overrides the ±2pp cross-night figure,
    92	  which came from multi-rack clusters) — residual noise is neighbor jobs,
    93	  clocks/thermals, and run-to-run scheduling jitter. The decision statistic
    94	  is the DIFFERENCE OF RUN-MEAN MFU, with run-level sd estimated from the
    95	  >=3 iteration-0 control draws and refreshed by later controls; per-step
    96	  points within a run are autocorrelated and are never treated as
    97	  independent replicates. KEEP requires >=2 treatment draws and >=2
    98	  same-session control draws (bracketing), delta > max(0.15 MFU,
    99	  3 x sd̂_run); a single C/T pair never keeps. Near-threshold deltas get
   100	  additional draws instead of a call. Periodic re-control draws guard
   101	  against slow drift across the campaign.
   102	- Fidelity gate for keeps: paired-window comparison; the loss guard compares
   103	  the FULL 15-step loss series against the paired control (max |delta|
   104	  within the bf16-noise band), not just the last step — data is
   105	  deterministic at a fixed restore step so the series pair pointwise. Any
   106	  keep that changes numerics beyond scheduling/layout gets flagged and needs
   107	  explicit reasoning about why the trajectory is unchanged.
   108	
   109	## Iteration accounting
   110	
   111	Per user: only hypotheses that reach the rack count toward the 24-iteration
   112	floor; review-killed ideas do not. Campaign runs 24 rack iterations or 24 h,
   113	whichever comes LAST. Iteration 0 = 30k-protocol baseline (control draw(s)).
   114	
   115	## Hypothesis ledger (priors from prior campaigns; refresh with a new profile)
   116	
   117	Excluded by constraint: fp8 dispatch wire #7665, MXFP8 (quantization);
   118	anything breaking hero restore.
   119	
   120	- H0 baseline+validation: >=3 control draws at 30k to establish the metric
   121	  and size noise. Draw 2 carries a profile tail (PROFILE_STEPS=3,
   122	  PROFILE_START_STEP=30021, past the scoring window) that (a) positively
   123	  confirms the `gpu_fa4_cute_wide` 128x64 kernel engaged on-rack — its
   124	  first rack outing, and cross-night MFU deltas cannot confirm dispatch —
   125	  and (b) refreshes the lever-sourcing profile (H1 folded in: no separate
   126	  profile arm). Draw 2 runs SCORE_MAX_STEP=28: the default early release at
   127	  +19 would cancel at 30019, before the 30021 trace (codex finding). If the
   128	  tail perturbs draw 2's scored window relative to draws 1/3, drop draw 2
   129	  from the noise estimate.
   130	- H1 profile refresh: FOLDED INTO H0 draw 2 (see above); prior profile
   131	  (dk-hero, pre-offload) showed marshal 2.55 s as top lever and a dense-MLP
   132	  leg that turned out to be architecture (2 shared experts + remat) — both
   133	  priors need re-confirmation on the offload/LHS/tile stack.
   134	- H2 marshal-step levers (SO-H2): reduce the ~2.55 s marshal leg — kernel or
   135	  layout changes in the dispatch/marshal path. Needs H1 to confirm the leg
   136	  survived the offload/LHS stack.
   137	- H3 shared-expert/dense leg scheduling: overlap the shared-expert compute
   138	  with ragged transport more aggressively; profile-informed.
   139	- H4 capacity-factor titration at the restored router: hero drops at cf 1.15
   140	  are ~5e-5 — far under any budget; cf 1.10/1.05 shrinks transport+GEMM
   141	  buffers. GATE (review finding): a cf keep changes training dynamics and
   142	  its drop cost varies with data over the whole run — a 15-step window
   143	  cannot bound it. Pre-declared acceptance: drop_fraction_max <= 1e-3 in
   144	  the window AND loss series within the control band; even then a cf keep
   145	  is flagged for explicit user sign-off before hero promotion, and the
   146	  prior loop note (2026-08-12: CF moves are frontier-walking) is quoted in
   147	  the decision. NOTE (codex): the 15-step window cannot establish whole-run
   148	  loss equivalence for a dynamics-touching change; H4's in-window
   149	  measurement establishes the THROUGHPUT effect only, and any promotion
   150	  decision belongs to the user.
   151	- H5 overlap-limit re-titration: DEAD AS A FLAG ARM on this base —
   152	  `offload_carry` force-sets overlap-limit 1 non-overridably and
   153	  `_apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults` silently strips conflicting
   154	  ARM_XLA_FLAGS (a flag-based treatment would run as an exact control).
   155	  Testing overlap>1 means changing the remat mode, which is a different
   156	  hypothesis (and the documented silent-corruption recipe with LHS +
   157	  offload — see activation-offload-lhs-overlap-race).
   158	- H6 slop-factor raise (user suggestion 2026-08-29, HIGH priority — cheap,
   159	  code-supported mechanism): `--xla_gpu_memory_limit_slop_factor=85` in
   160	  train.py flag_defaults was calibrated for the pre-offload posture (default
   161	  95 sized the temp arena at 125.7 GiB > the 120.2 GiB the pool can serve;
   162	  85 -> 113.6 GiB), and the comment itself says a smaller arena makes
   163	  HloRematerialization recompute more of the step. The carry offload since
   164	  dropped peak from ~138 to ~116.5 GiB, so the remat pressure the arena cap
   165	  creates may now be pure waste. The flag IS overridable via inherited
   166	  XLA_FLAGS (verified: only overlap-limit and ragged-required flags are
   167	  forced), so treatment = ARM_XLA_FLAGS with slop 90 (arena ~120.2, at the
   168	  documented bound; ladder to 88 on OOM, 92/95 on green+gain). Fidelity:
   169	  remat changes recompute scheduling only. Engagement check: peak-HBM
   170	  metric must RISE vs control (less remat holds more live); an unchanged
   171	  peak means the flag did not engage regardless of the MFU reading. Failure
   172	  mode is a named first-step NCCL allocation abort — cheap crash, ladder
   173	  down.
   174	- H7 attn-offload re-add: 23.14 but 3x stdev and the host allocation lands as
   175	  one contiguous 72.56 GiB block regardless of HOST_MEM_LIMIT_GB — ledger
   176	  says dead unless pod memory grows.
   177	- H8 marin_ep_mgpu_fused transport on this stack: mok-goal showed 22.83 vs
   178	  22.30 control at 18k restore with 16x fewer drops, but needs the kmax128
   179	  wheel (conflicts with the pinned c9526e wheel) and the backend isn't on
   180	  this branch. Big integration; park unless transport dominates H1 profile
   181	  and cheaper levers dry up.
   182	- Dead-lever ledger (do not re-run without new evidence): PGLE (null),
   183	  command buffers at hero shape (+7.5 GiB, negative), FA4 backward tile
   184	  (hard-locked 64x64), 128x128 forward tile (2.5x regression), fused shared
   185	  experts (~0), small-param FSDP (negative), unchaining per-chunk a2a
   186	  buffers (-0.13), NVLS/SHARP env (null), fa4-lse primal output (moot: XLA
   187	  already device-saves FA4 out/lse).
   188	
   189	## Decision rules
   190	
   191	- keep: same-night pair shows treatment > control by >0.15 MFU with guards
   192	  clean → commit stays, becomes the new control for later iterations.
   193	- discard: delta <= noise or guards fail → revert commit.
   194	- crash/invalid: watchdog kill, preemption, incomplete window → arm is
   195	  invalid, does not consume the hypothesis; resubmit once before discarding.
   196	- Every keep is re-verified implicitly by later same-night controls (the
   197	  control arm always runs the current kept stack).
   198	
   199	## Review protocol
   200	
   201	Before rack time each experiment's design gets adversarial review from (a) a
   202	fable agent and (b) `codex exec` CLI, both against the pinned worktree
   203	(`codex exec` needs stdin closed; `--base` incompatible with a custom
   204	prompt). Findings addressed or refuted in writing in the logbook before
   205	submission. Reviews of iteration N+1 overlap with rack time of iteration N.
    75	    "XLA_PYTHON_CLIENT_ALLOCATOR": "cuda_async",
    76	    # `cuda_async` reads this fraction as the mempool release threshold, and PJRT sizes the
    77	    # collective pool at the remaining `(1 - fraction) x 184.3 GiB`. 0.75 sets a 138.2 GiB
    78	    # threshold and leaves ~46 GiB for collectives, above the ~28.5 GiB NCCL, cuBLAS, and the
    79	    # CUDA context hold outside the allocator. A higher fraction strands memory the step never
    80	    # uses, because the startup preallocation probe commits the whole threshold at once: 0.83
    81	    # pins 153.0 GiB in the pool and leaves under 3 GiB free on the device, so the arena
    82	    # allocation below has no room to remap into.
    83	    "XLA_PYTHON_CLIENT_MEM_FRACTION": "0.75",
    84	}
    85	XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG = "--xla_gpu_experimental_parallel_collective_overlap_limit"
    86	DEFAULT_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT = 4
    87	DEFAULT_DROPLESS_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION: MoeImplementation = "sonic_cute"
    88	# Full inline norm watch failed with overlap 4. Overlap 1 completed the selected full-watch gate.
    89	INLINE_WATCH_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT = 1
    90	# The ragged transport wants the opposite scheduling posture from the fixed and pooled ones. Its
    91	# dispatch and combine form one long dependent chain, so admitting several concurrent collectives
    92	# only contends for the SMs the transport itself needs.
    93	RAGGED_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT = 1
    94	# The layer-carry offload, the latency-hiding scheduler, and an overlap limit above 1 corrupt
    95	# training together, and no pair of the three does: the reloaded residual races its consumer in
    96	# backward, so a restored run reports a first-step loss near 9.5 instead of its checkpoint's
    97	# (#8317). Overlap 4 gained 0.07 MFU, so the offload forces this value rather than defaulting it.
    98	OFFLOAD_CARRY_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT = 1
    99	RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION = "ragged_all_to_all"
   100	# TODO(https://github.com/marin-community/marin/issues/5675): Re-enable XLA GPU
   101	# command buffers after the CUDA graph failure is fixed.
   102	XLA_DISABLE_GPU_COMMAND_BUFFER_FLAG = "--xla_gpu_enable_command_buffer="
   103	_RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAG_NAMES = frozenset(flag.partition("=")[0] for flag in RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAGS)
   104	PJRT_DISTRIBUTION = "jax-cuda13-pjrt"
   105	_FP32_POLICY = jmp.get_policy("params=float32,compute=float32,output=float32")

   107	
   108	class WatchMode(StrEnum):
   109	    """Where a watched training step computes gradient and parameter statistics."""
   110	
   111	    INLINE = "inline"
   112	    DIAGNOSTIC = "diagnostic"
   113	
   114	
   115	class MasterParamMode(StrEnum):
   116	    """Where the authoritative fp32 weights live.
   117	
   118	    DEVICE keeps them as the device params themselves, with no separate master copy;
   119	    FP32_PINNED_HOST keeps a pinned-host fp32 master while the device params are its bf16 cast.
   120	    """
   121	
   122	    DEVICE = "device"
   123	    FP32_PINNED_HOST = "fp32_pinned_host"
   124	
   125	
   126	class TrainingDataMode(StrEnum):
   127	    """Source of training batches."""
   128	
   129	    MIXTURE = "mixture"
   130	    SYNTHETIC = "synthetic"
   131	
   132	
   133	def restore_template_from(state):
   134	    """ShapeDtypeStructs carrying each leaf's concrete sharding, releasing the leaves.
   135	
   136	    `jax.eval_shape` drops the `pinned_host` memory kind that `initial_state` puts on
   137	    offloaded optimizer state, which is most of a hero checkpoint. Reading the sharding off a
   138	    built state keeps it.
   139	    """
   140	    template = jax.tree.map(
   141	        lambda leaf: (
   142	            jax.ShapeDtypeStruct(leaf.shape, leaf.dtype, sharding=leaf.sharding) if isinstance(leaf, jax.Array) else leaf
   143	        ),
   144	        state,
   145	    )
   146	    jax.tree.map(lambda leaf: leaf.delete() if isinstance(leaf, jax.Array) else None, state)
   147	    gc.collect()
   148	    return template
   149	
   150	
   151	def checkpoint_stores_master(candidate: str) -> bool:
   152	    """Whether checkpoint ``candidate``'s manifest lists fp32 master parameters.
   153	
   154	    A missing manifest raises ``FileNotFoundError``, which restore treats like any other
   155	    unreadable candidate.
   156	    """
   157	    manifest = read_manifest(candidate)
   158	    if manifest is None:
   159	        raise FileNotFoundError(f"{candidate} has no manifest.json, so its layout cannot be read")
   160	    markers = (MASTER_PARAMS_KEY, f"{LEGACY_STATE_KEY}/{MASTER_PARAMS_KEY}")
   161	    return any(path == marker or path.startswith(marker + "/") for path in manifest.array_paths for marker in markers)
   162	
   163	
   164	def template_for_candidate_layout(
   165	    state: "GrugTrainState", candidate: str, run_mode: MasterParamMode
   166	) -> "GrugTrainState":
   167	    """Pick the template restore reads checkpoint ``candidate`` with.
   168	
   169	    Restore reads only the leaves the template names and takes each dtype from storage, checking
   170	    neither against the checkpoint, so reading a master-bearing checkpoint with the run's own
   171	    master-less template would silently return the bf16 compute copy. When the layouts match the
   172	    template is ``state`` itself. A master-bearing checkpoint read by a master-less run migrates
   173	    in process: the run's device fp32 ``params`` template is presented under ``master_params``, so
   174	    the checkpoint's authoritative fp32 master loads directly into it and the bf16 copy goes
   175	    unread; ``take_master_as_params`` then moves it back, and the next save writes the new layout.
   176	    """
   177	    has_master = checkpoint_stores_master(candidate)
   178	    if has_master == (run_mode != MasterParamMode.DEVICE):
   179	        return state
   180	    if not has_master:
   181	        raise ValueError(
   182	            f"checkpoint {candidate} stores no master parameters, but this run trains with {run_mode}. "
   183	            "Synthesizing a master from stored weights is not a conversion this supports."
   184	        )
   185	    logger.info("Checkpoint %s stores a pinned-host fp32 master; restoring it as the parameters.", candidate)
   186	    return dataclasses.replace(state, params=None, master_params=state.params)
   187	
   188	
   189	def take_master_as_params(state: "GrugTrainState") -> "GrugTrainState":
   190	    """Move a master restored through ``template_for_candidate_layout`` into ``params``."""
   191	    if state.master_params is None:
   192	        return state
   193	    return dataclasses.replace(state, params=state.master_params, master_params=None)
   194	
   195	
   196	def _apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults(
   197	    *,
   198	    inline_watch_enabled: bool,
   199	    moe_implementation: MoeImplementation | None,
   200	    remat_mode: RematMode,
   201	    processes_per_task: int = 1,
   202	) -> None:
   203	    env_defaults = dict(HERO_EP_RUNTIME_ENV)
   204	    if processes_per_task > 1:
   205	        # With one process per GPU, the per-process CUPTI sessions collide with each
   206	        # other and with CoreWeave's DCGM, so PGLE cannot profile and its recompile
   207	        # machinery only adds failure modes. Default it off; an explicit env wins.
   208	        env_defaults["JAX_ENABLE_PGLE"] = "false"
   209	    for name, value in env_defaults.items():
   210	        os.environ.setdefault(name, value)
   211	    xla_flags = os.environ.get("XLA_FLAGS", "").split()
   212	    ragged = moe_implementation == RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION
   213	    if ragged:
   214	        overlap_limit = RAGGED_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   215	    elif inline_watch_enabled:
   216	        overlap_limit = INLINE_WATCH_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   217	    else:
   218	        overlap_limit = DEFAULT_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   219	    # The scheduler's longer buffer live ranges fit on the ragged transport only once the layer
   220	    # carry leaves HBM. Without that offload its first-step NCCL allocations fail.
   221	    latency_hiding = not ragged or remat_mode == "offload_carry"
   222	    flag_defaults = (
   223	        f"{XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG}={overlap_limit}",
   224	        f"--xla_gpu_enable_latency_hiding_scheduler={'true' if latency_hiding else 'false'}",
   225	        # The scheduler sizes the single `jit_train_step` temp arena against this percentage of
   226	        # its memory budget, roughly `133.6 GiB x percentage`. The pool holds 138.2 GiB and
   227	        # persistent state occupies 18.1 GiB of it, so an arena above 120.2 GiB cannot be served
   228	        # from pool free space and forces a fresh mapping against the ~17 GiB of physical memory
   229	        # outside the pool. The default 95 asks for 125.7 GiB and fails that way. 85 sizes the
   230	        # arena at 113.6 GiB, leaving enough slack for per-node variation in fragmentation. A
   231	        # lower percentage costs throughput, because a smaller arena makes `HloRematerialization`
   232	        # recompute more of the step.
   233	        "--xla_gpu_memory_limit_slop_factor=85",
   234	        XLA_DISABLE_GPU_COMMAND_BUFFER_FLAG,
   235	    )
   236	    explicit_names = {flag.partition("=")[0] for flag in xla_flags}
   237	    xla_flags.extend(flag for flag in flag_defaults if flag.partition("=")[0] not in explicit_names)
   238	    if remat_mode == "offload_carry":
   239	        # Not overridable, unlike the defaults above, including the scheduler flag it pairs with.
   240	        # Both flags can be inherited from another recipe, but only this one fails silently: a
   241	        # wrong overlap limit corrupts training, while an inherited scheduler setting either
   242	        # aborts in NCCL's first-step allocation and names itself, or runs the slower posture.
   243	        xla_flags = [f for f in xla_flags if f.partition("=")[0] != XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG]
   244	        xla_flags.append(f"{XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG}={OFFLOAD_CARRY_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT}")
   245	    if ragged:
   246	        # Unlike the defaults above, these are not overridable. Selecting the host-launched
   247	        # one-shot kernel needs both flags cleared together plus a splits-per-peer count this
   248	        # branch no longer carries, so honoring a partial override would run a configuration
   249	        # nothing here measures. Drop any conflicting entry rather than relying on which
   250	        # occurrence XLA's parser keeps.
   251	        xla_flags = [f for f in xla_flags if f.partition("=")[0] not in _RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAG_NAMES]
   252	        xla_flags.extend(RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAGS)
   253	    os.environ["XLA_FLAGS"] = " ".join(xla_flags)
   254	
   255	
   590	    flops_per_example = 3 * flops_per_token * model_config.max_seq_len
   591	
   592	    flops_summary: dict[str, float] = {
   593	        "throughput/flops_per_token_analytic": flops_per_token,
   594	        "throughput/flops_per_example_analytic": flops_per_example,
   595	    }
   596	
   597	    return flops_per_example, flops_summary
   598	
   599	
   600	def log_device_memory(step_info) -> None:
   601	    """Log this process's local-device HBM peak, live bytes, and allocator limit in GiB.
   602	
   603	    The EP hero had no peak-HBM telemetry, which makes a whole class of result unreadable: XLA's
   604	    ``HloRematerialization`` engages only when peak crosses the allocator limit, so a config change
   605	    that moves peak across that boundary produces an MFU step change that has nothing to do with the
   606	    change itself. Issue #8054 traced its own +9.08% headline to exactly this -- 3.69 GiB of peak
   607	    took it under the limit and switched remat off -- and the win fell to +3.31% once one process
   608	    per GPU put 8.79 GiB back. Any ablation that moves activation memory needs this logged, or its
   609	    rungs cannot be told apart from allocator-limit crossings.
   610	
   611	    Ported from ``experiments/grug/moe_hero_fsdp/train.py``.
   612	    """
   613	    stats = jax.local_devices()[0].memory_stats()
   614	    levanter.tracker.log(
   615	        {
   616	            "memory/peak_gib": stats["peak_bytes_in_use"] / 1024**3,
   617	            "memory/in_use_gib": stats["bytes_in_use"] / 1024**3,
   618	            "memory/limit_gib": stats["bytes_limit"] / 1024**3,
   619	        },
   620	        step=step_info.step,
   621	    )
   622	
   623	
   624	def _make_mixture_stage_callback(train_dataset: MixtureDataset, batch_schedule: BatchSchedule):
   625	    last_mixture_stage = -1
  1080	            every=log_every,
  1081	        )
  1082	        state_callbacks.add_hook(callbacks.pbar_logger(total=stop_step), every=log_every)
  1083	        state_callbacks.add_hook(callbacks.log_step_info(stop_step), every=log_every)
  1084	        if profiler_enabled:
  1085	            state_callbacks.add_hook(
  1086	                profiler_cfg.build(
  1087	                    str(trainer.log_dir / run_id / "profiler"),
  1088	                    run_id=run_id,
  1089	                    num_steps=profiler_num_steps,
  1090	                ),
  1091	                every=1,
  1092	            )
  1093	        if train_dataset is not None:
  1094	            state_callbacks.add_hook(_make_mixture_stage_callback(train_dataset, batch_schedule), every=1)
  1095	        state_callbacks.add_hook(log_device_memory, every=1)
  1096	        if evaluator is not None and eval_cfg is not None:
  1097	            interval = eval_cfg.steps_per_eval
  1098	            eval_ema = eval_cfg.eval_ema and config.trainer.ema_beta is not None
  1099	            if eval_cfg.eval_current or eval_ema:
  1100	                tagged_eval_hook = cb_tagged_evaluate(
  1101	                    evaluator,
  1102	                    prefix=eval_cfg.prefix,
  1103	                    eval_current=eval_cfg.eval_current,
  1104	                    eval_ema=eval_ema,
  1105	                )
  1106	                eval_hooks: list[Callable[..., None]] = [tagged_eval_hook]
  1107	                if dropless_evaluator is not None and dropless_eval_mesh is not None:
  1108	                    # The training loop runs under `set_mesh(mesh)` (expert-parallel). The dropless
  1109	                    # evaluator runs under the expert-collapsed mesh, so the model params -- sharded on
  1110	                    # the train mesh -- must be resharded onto the eval mesh before its eval jit (JAX
  1235	                ):
  1236	                    checkpointer.on_step(tree=state, step=int(state.step), force=True)
  1237	                    checkpointer.wait_until_finished()
  1238	        finally:
  1239	            state_callbacks.emit_event(callbacks.ProgressEvent.TRAINING_FINISHED)
  1240	
  1241	    levanter.tracker.current_tracker().finish()
  1242	
  1243	
  1244	def run_grug(config: GrugRunConfig) -> None:
  1245	    """Dispatch grug training through Fray jobs."""
  1246	    trainer = config.trainer.trainer
  1247	    if trainer.id is None:
  1248	        raise ValueError("trainer.id must be set before dispatching grug training.")
  1249	
  1250	    # Dispatch snapshots os.environ for the child task, so apply the hero defaults first.
  1251	    inline_watch_enabled = trainer.watch.is_enabled and config.trainer.watch_mode == WatchMode.INLINE
  1252	    _apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults(
  1253	        inline_watch_enabled=inline_watch_enabled,
  1254	        processes_per_task=config.processes_per_task,
  1255	        moe_implementation=config.model.moe_implementation,
  1256	        remat_mode=config.model.remat_mode,
  1257	    )
  1258	    dispatch_grug_training_run(
  1259	        run_id=trainer.id,
  1260	        config=config,
  1261	        local_entrypoint=_run_grug_local,
  1262	        resources=config.resources,
  1263	        processes_per_task=config.processes_per_task,
  1264	        max_retries_failure=config.max_retries_failure,
  1265	        max_task_failures=config.max_task_failures,
     1	# Copyright The Marin Authors
     2	# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     3	
     4	import logging
     5	import os
     6	import re
     7	from collections.abc import Callable
     8	from typing import TypeVar
     9	
    10	from fray.cluster import ResourceConfig
    11	from fray.current_client import current_client
    12	from fray.types import Entrypoint, JobRequest, create_environment
    13	from iris.rpc.proto_display import priority_band_value
    14	from marin.training.run_environment import extras_for_resources
    15	from marin.training.training import resolve_training_env
    16	
    17	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    18	
    19	ConfigT = TypeVar("ConfigT")
    20	
    21	# `JobRequest.priority` is the Iris priority band as a bare int. INHERIT is Iris's own default.
    22	INHERIT_PRIORITY = priority_band_value("inherit")
    23	
    24	# Runtime-tuning env vars forwarded from the dispatcher to the train tasks.
    25	# Iris tasks don't inherit the submitter's shell, so anything the launcher was
    26	# given (e.g. `iris job run -e XLA_FLAGS ...`) must be re-exported explicitly.
    27	# JAX_PLATFORMS is excluded: the dispatcher runs CPU-only and its value must
    28	# not leak onto accelerator tasks.
    29	_FORWARDED_ENV_PREFIXES = ("XLA_", "LIBTPU_INIT_ARGS", "NCCL_", "JAX_", "MALLOC_")
    30	_FORWARDED_ENV_NAMES = ("LD_PRELOAD",)
    31	_FORWARDED_ENV_EXCLUDE = ("JAX_PLATFORMS",)
    32	
    33	
    34	def _forwarded_env_vars() -> dict[str, str]:
    35	    return {
    36	        k: v
    37	        for k, v in os.environ.items()
    38	        if (k in _FORWARDED_ENV_NAMES or k.startswith(_FORWARDED_ENV_PREFIXES)) and k not in _FORWARDED_ENV_EXCLUDE
    39	    }
    40	
    41	
    42	def _safe_job_suffix(run_id: str) -> str:
    43	    """Sanitize run IDs into Fray/Iris-safe job-name suffixes."""
    44	    return re.sub(r"[^A-Za-z0-9_.-]+", "-", run_id)
    45	
    46	
    47	def dispatch_grug_training_run(
    48	    *,
    49	    run_id: str,
    50	    config: ConfigT,
    51	    local_entrypoint: Callable[[ConfigT], None],
    52	    resources: ResourceConfig,
    53	    max_retries_failure: int = 3,
    54	    max_task_failures: int = 10,
    55	    processes_per_task: int = 1,
    56	    priority: int = INHERIT_PRIORITY,
    57	) -> None:
    58	    """Submit a grug train entrypoint through Fray and wait for completion.
    59	
    60	    ``INHERIT_PRIORITY`` takes the submitting job's band, or ``interactive`` when the submitter is
    61	    not itself an Iris job -- which is the case for a launcher run from a dev box.
    62	
    63	    ``max_retries_failure`` is the per-task retry budget and ``max_task_failures`` is the
    64	    cumulative one. The job fails when either is exhausted, so raise the two together: a large
    65	    per-task budget under a small cumulative one still ends the job at the cumulative limit.
    66	    """
    67	    safe_run_id = _safe_job_suffix(run_id)
    68	    env_vars = resolve_training_env(base_env=_forwarded_env_vars(), resources=resources)
    69	    request = JobRequest(
    70	        name=f"grug-train-{safe_run_id}",
    71	        entrypoint=Entrypoint.from_callable(local_entrypoint, args=[config]),
    72	        resources=resources,
    73	        environment=create_environment(env_vars=env_vars, extras=extras_for_resources(resources)),
    74	        max_retries_failure=max_retries_failure,
    75	        max_task_failures=max_task_failures,
    76	        processes_per_task=processes_per_task,
    77	        priority=priority,
    78	    )
    79	    logger.info("Dispatching grug training via Fray: %s", request.name)
    80	    job = current_client().submit(request)
    81	    job.wait(raise_on_failure=True)
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Submit one hero EP64 arm for the 8753-mfu loop (research/mcwitt/8753-mfu-loop, #8753 head base).
     3	#
     4	# Protocol (fixed for every iteration so arms stay comparable; see DESIGN.md):
     5	#   - one NVL72 rack on cw-us-east-08a, PRODUCTION priority allowed with job runtime <1h
     6	#     (user 2026-08-28): ARM_TIMEOUT 3500s outer backstop, watchdog.sh COMPILE_CEILING=1800 +
     7	#     STEP_BUDGET=1200 inner caps (20-min post-compile cap)
     8	#   - restore from the live hero's step-30000 checkpoint, mixture data (trained-router routing);
     9	#     NUM_STEPS is the ABSOLUTE stop step (checkpoint step + window), never a relative count
    10	#   - no checkpoints, no eval; profiler off on SCORED arms (a profile tail on an otherwise-scored
    11	#     draw is allowed: PROFILE_STEPS>0 with PROFILE_START_STEP as an ABSOLUTE step past the
    12	#     scoring window, e.g. 30021)
    13	#   - no MARIN_PJRT_WHEEL overlay: the branch's uv.lock pins jax_cuda13_pjrt 0.11.1+marin.c9526e8c0272
    14	#   - JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR rotated per run id (clique-init deadlock dodge); a RESUBMITTED
    15	#     arm must use a fresh RID (and VERSION): reusing one merges W&B histories, reuses the
    16	#     leader-populated compile cache (clique-deadlock recipe), and can vacuously reuse artifacts
    17	#
    18	# usage: RID=<run-id> VERSION=<calver> [REPO=<worktree>] [EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS="..."] arm.sh
    19	set -euo pipefail
    20	
    21	: "${WANDB_API_KEY:?set WANDB_API_KEY}"
    22	RID="${RID:?set RID}"
    23	VERSION="${VERSION:?set VERSION -- bump it per arm or the artifact layer reuses the last run}"
    24	
    25	# The watchdog addresses jobs at /mwittmann/...; make the submission land there no matter what
    26	# the caller's shell has. (-e below only sets the remote env.)
    27	export IRIS_USER=mwittmann
    28	
    29	LOOP_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
    30	REPO="${REPO:-$(cd "${LOOP_DIR}/../.." && pwd)}"
    31	
    32	# Iris bundles the working tree, not HEAD: uncommitted edits (including another session writing
    33	# to a shared checkout) would ship silently and break arm attribution. Only the loop dir's DATA
    34	# files (logs, tsv, review transcripts) are exempt; uncommitted protocol scripts count as dirty,
    35	# since arms.tsv records HEAD as the arm's provenance.
    36	dirty="$(git -C "$REPO" status --porcelain | grep -Ev "autoresearch/.*\.(log|out|txt|tsv)$" || true)"
    37	if [ -z "${ALLOW_DIRTY:-}" ] && [ -n "$dirty" ]; then
    38	  echo "refusing to submit from a dirty tree (set ALLOW_DIRTY=1 to override):" >&2
    39	  echo "$dirty" >&2
    40	  exit 1
    41	fi
    42	
    43	# One RID/VERSION per submission, ever: a reused RID merges W&B histories, resurrects the
    44	# leader-populated compile cache (clique-deadlock recipe), and can vacuously reuse artifacts.
    45	if [ -f "${LOOP_DIR}/arms.tsv" ] && awk -F'\t' -v rid="$RID" -v ver="$VERSION" \
    46	    'NR>1 && ($1==rid || $3==ver) {found=1} END {exit !found}' "${LOOP_DIR}/arms.tsv"; then
    47	  echo "RID ${RID} or VERSION ${VERSION} already appears in arms.tsv; pick fresh ones" >&2
    48	  exit 1
    49	fi
    50	
    51	PRIORITY="${PRIORITY:-production}"
    52	ARM_TIMEOUT="${ARM_TIMEOUT:-3500}"
    53	NUM_STEPS="${NUM_STEPS:-30030}"
    54	TRAINING_DATA="${TRAINING_DATA:-mixture}"
    55	MASTER_PARAMS="${MASTER_PARAMS:-device}"
    56	MOE_IMPL="${MOE_IMPL:-ragged_all_to_all}"
    57	RESTORE_FROM="${RESTORE_FROM:-s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin/grug/hero-12d8b6f0-dee637/2026.08.19.2/checkpoints/step-30000}"
    58	RESTORE_ARGS=()
    59	if [ -n "$RESTORE_FROM" ]; then
    60	  RESTORE_ARGS=(--restore-from "$RESTORE_FROM")
    61	fi
    62	# The learning-rate schedule's length; fixed across arms so losses stay comparable.
    63	SCHEDULE_STEPS=4470000
    64	
    65	read -r -a EXTRA <<<"${EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS:-}"
    66	
    67	cd "$REPO"
    68	uv run iris --config lib/iris/config/marin.yaml job run \
    69	  --no-wait --enable-extra-resources \
    70	  --target-cluster cw-us-east-08a --priority "${PRIORITY}" \
    71	  --cpu 2 --memory 8GB --disk 32GB --timeout "${ARM_TIMEOUT}" \
    72	  --job-name "${RID}-coord" \
    73	  -e IRIS_USER mwittmann \
    74	  -e WANDB_API_KEY "${WANDB_API_KEY}" \
    75	  -e WANDB_PROJECT marin_moe \
    76	  -e MARIN_PREFIX s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin \
    77	  -e IRIS_PORT_JAX 32703 \
    78	  -e AWS_MAX_ATTEMPTS 25 -e AWS_RETRY_MODE adaptive \
    79	  -e JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR "s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin/tmp/ttl=30d/jaxcache/${RID}" \
    80	  -e XLA_FLAGS "${ARM_XLA_FLAGS:-}" \
    81	  -- python -m experiments.grug.moe_hero_ep.launch_diagnostics \
    82	     --run-id "${RID}" \
    83	     --dp-racks 1 --num-steps "${NUM_STEPS}" --schedule-steps "${SCHEDULE_STEPS}" \
    84	     --capacity-factor 1.15 \
    85	     --moe-implementation "${MOE_IMPL}" \
    86	     --processes-per-task 4 \
    87	     --master-params "${MASTER_PARAMS}" \
    88	     --training-data "${TRAINING_DATA}" \
    89	     --profile-steps "${PROFILE_STEPS:-0}" --profile-start-step "${PROFILE_START_STEP:-30021}" \
    90	     --watch-interval 0 --eval-every 0 \
    91	     --no-save-checkpoints \
    92	     "${RESTORE_ARGS[@]}" \
    93	     "${EXTRA[@]}" \
    94	     --version "${VERSION}" --run >"${LOOP_DIR}/${RID}-submit.log" 2>&1 \
    95	  || { echo "submit FAILED, tail of ${RID}-submit.log:" >&2; tail -20 "${LOOP_DIR}/${RID}-submit.log" >&2; exit 1; }
    96	
    97	printf '%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\n' \
    98	  "${RID}" "$(git -C "$REPO" rev-parse --short HEAD)" "${VERSION}" "${MOE_IMPL}" "${TRAINING_DATA}" \
    99	  "${MASTER_PARAMS}" "${SCHEDULE_STEPS}" "${ARM_TIMEOUT}" "${RESTORE_FROM:-none}" \
   100	  "${EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS:-none}" >> "${LOOP_DIR}/arms.tsv"
   101	
   102	echo "submitted ${RID} at ${PRIORITY} priority (timeout ${ARM_TIMEOUT}s, commit $(git -C "$REPO" rev-parse --short HEAD), data ${TRAINING_DATA}, extra: ${EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS:-none})"
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Out-of-process runtime cap, early release, and anomaly alerting for one arm.
     3	#
     4	# Exits (each prints one WATCHDOG marker line; the harness alerts on process exit, so exiting
     5	# fast IS the alert):
     6	#   1. Step budget: cancel STEP_BUDGET seconds after the first step this watch observes. Compile
     7	#      time varies run to run; an arm that pays a slow compile still gets its full measurement
     8	#      window, but an arm whose change makes *steps* slow gets no extra rack time.
     9	#   2. Compile ceiling: cancel if no step has appeared COMPILE_CEILING seconds after submission.
    10	#   3. Early release: cancel as soon as W&B shows the last step the scoring window needs.
    11	#   4. Preemption or task failure: `job describe` publishes failures= and preemptions= counters.
    12	#      Either going nonzero invalidates the measurement (a rescheduled gang recompiles and the
    13	#      step timings straddle the gap), so cancel immediately instead of letting the retry burn
    14	#      the ceiling.
    15	#   5. Coordinator death before the train job exists (e.g. a config guard raising at dispatch):
    16	#      previously this burned the whole compile ceiling; now it exits within one poll.
    17	# Iris's own --timeout on the coord job is the outer backstop; it must cover
    18	# COMPILE_CEILING + STEP_BUDGET. This watchdog runs in a separate process so a wedged CLI or a
    19	# lost session cannot defeat it.
    20	#
    21	# State polls are cheap (one describe RPC), so they run every POLL=20s; the W&B step query is
    22	# heavier and runs every third poll.
    23	#
    24	# usage: RID=<run-id> [STEP_BUDGET=900] [COMPILE_CEILING=1200] [SCORE_MAX_STEP=19] watchdog.sh
    25	set -uo pipefail
    26	
    27	RID="${RID:?set RID}"
    28	STEP_BUDGET="${STEP_BUDGET:-1200}"
    29	COMPILE_CEILING="${COMPILE_CEILING:-1800}"
    30	SCORE_MAX_STEP="${SCORE_MAX_STEP:-19}"
    31	POLL="${POLL:-20}"
    32	STEP_POLL_EVERY="${STEP_POLL_EVERY:-3}"
    33	# A campaign arm restores from step-30000; a first step below this means restore silently fell
    34	# back to scratch (or the wrong checkpoint) and the whole measurement is invalid.
    35	EXPECTED_MIN_FIRST_STEP="${EXPECTED_MIN_FIRST_STEP:-30000}"
    36	
    37	LOOP_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
    38	REPO="$(cd "${LOOP_DIR}/../.." && pwd)"
    39	IRIS=(uv run iris --config lib/iris/config/marin.yaml)
    40	COORD="/mwittmann/${RID}-coord"
    41	TRAIN="${COORD}/grug-train-${RID}"
    42	start=$(date +%s)
    43	# A production submission may queue behind another job, so the compile ceiling starts when the
    44	# train job is first observed running, not at submission. QUEUE_CEILING bounds the pending phase.
    45	QUEUE_CEILING="${QUEUE_CEILING:-10800}"
    46	deadline=$(( start + QUEUE_CEILING ))
    47	run_seen=-1
    48	first_step=-1
    49	steps=-1
    50	tick=0
    51	
    52	cancel_arm() {  # cancel_arm <reason>
    53	  echo "$(date -u +%H:%M:%S) cancelling ${COORD}: $1"
    54	  # Cancel the train job by its own path as well as through the coordinator. Iris's --timeout may
    55	  # already have taken the coordinator down, and a cancel routed only through a dead parent is
    56	  # exactly how a 16-node child gets orphaned on the rack.
    57	  ( cd "$REPO" && timeout 300 "${IRIS[@]}" job cancel "$TRAIN" 2>&1 | tail -2 )
    58	  ( cd "$REPO" && timeout 300 "${IRIS[@]}" job cancel "$COORD" 2>&1 | tail -2 )
    59	  # A cancel that silently failed leaves a 16-node gang holding the rack while the supervisor
    60	  # submits the next arm. Verify the train job actually reached a terminal state; if it does not,
    61	  # say so loudly and exit nonzero so the supervisor stops instead of stacking arms.
    62	  for _ in 1 2 3 4 5 6; do
    63	    read -r vstate _ _ <<<"$(state_line_of "$TRAIN")" || true
    64	    case "${vstate:-gone}" in
    65	      succeeded|killed|cancelled|*failed|gone) return 0;;
    66	    esac
    67	    sleep 10
    68	  done
    69	  echo "WATCHDOG CANCEL-UNVERIFIED train=${vstate:-?} -- do NOT submit the next arm until the rack is confirmed free"
    70	  exit 2
    71	}
    72	
    73	state_line_of() {  # state_line_of <job path> -> "state failures preemptions" (empty if absent)
    74	  ( cd "$REPO" && timeout 120 "${IRIS[@]}" job describe "$1" 2>/dev/null \
    75	      | sed -n 's/^State: \([a-z_]*\).*failures=\([0-9]*\).*preemptions=\([0-9]*\).*/\1 \2 \3/p' | head -1 )
    76	}
    77	
    78	max_step() {
    79	  ( cd "$REPO" && timeout 180 uv run --with wandb python - "$RID" <<'PY' 2>/dev/null
    80	import sys, wandb
    81	try:
    82	    run = wandb.Api().run(f"marin-community/marin_moe/{sys.argv[1]}")
    83	except Exception:
    84	    print(-2); raise SystemExit
    85	steps = [row.get("_step", -1) for row in run.scan_history(keys=["_step", "throughput/mfu"])]
    86	print(max(steps) if steps else -1)
    87	PY
    88	  )
    89	}
    90	
    91	while :; do
    92	  now=$(date +%s)
    93	  if [ "$now" -ge "$deadline" ]; then
    94	    if [ "${first_step:--1}" -ge 0 ] 2>/dev/null; then
    95	      cancel_arm "step budget ${STEP_BUDGET}s past first step reached"
    96	      echo "WATCHDOG hard-cap step-budget"
    97	    else
    98	      if [ "${run_seen:--1}" -ge 0 ]; then
    99	        cancel_arm "no step within the ${COMPILE_CEILING}s compile ceiling"
   100	        echo "WATCHDOG hard-cap compile-ceiling"
   101	      else
   102	        cancel_arm "still queued after ${QUEUE_CEILING}s"
   103	        echo "WATCHDOG hard-cap queue-ceiling"
   104	      fi
   105	    fi
   106	    exit 0
   107	  fi
   108	
   109	  read -r train_state train_failures train_preemptions <<<"$(state_line_of "$TRAIN")" || true
   110	  if [ -z "${train_state:-}" ]; then
   111	    # No train job yet: the coordinator is either still dispatching or died before dispatch.
   112	    read -r coord_state coord_failures coord_preemptions <<<"$(state_line_of "$COORD")" || true
   113	    case "${coord_state:-}" in
   114	      succeeded|killed|cancelled|*failed)
   115	        cancel_arm "coordinator ${coord_state} before the train job existed"
   116	        echo "WATCHDOG coord-terminal state=${coord_state} failures=${coord_failures:-?}"
   117	        exit 0;;
   118	    esac
   119	  fi
   120	  if [ "${run_seen:--1}" -lt 0 ] && [ "${first_step:--1}" -lt 0 ] && [ "${train_state:-}" = "running" ]; then
   121	    # Iris can report a gang "running" while another job still holds the rack; the W&B run is
   122	    # only created once distributed init actually completes, so gate the compile clock on it.
   123	    # The first_step guard keeps this from re-arming the longer compile deadline over an
   124	    # already-started step budget when the run-existence probe failed transiently earlier.
   125	    probe="$(max_step)"
   126	    if [ "${probe:--2}" -ge -1 ] 2>/dev/null; then
   127	      run_seen=$now
   128	      deadline=$(( now + COMPILE_CEILING ))
   129	      echo "$(date -u +%H:%M:%S) gang up (wandb run exists), compile ceiling ${COMPILE_CEILING}s starts now"
   130	    fi
   131	  fi
   132	  if [ "${train_preemptions:-0}" -gt 0 ] 2>/dev/null; then
   133	    cancel_arm "train job preempted (preemptions=${train_preemptions})"
   134	    echo "WATCHDOG preempted train=${train_state} preemptions=${train_preemptions} step=${steps}"
   135	    exit 0
   136	  fi
   137	  if [ "${train_failures:-0}" -gt 0 ] 2>/dev/null; then
   138	    cancel_arm "train task failed (failures=${train_failures})"
   139	    echo "WATCHDOG task-failed train=${train_state} failures=${train_failures} step=${steps}"
   140	    exit 0
   141	  fi
   142	  case "${train_state:-}" in
   143	    succeeded|killed|cancelled|*failed)
   144	      echo "WATCHDOG terminal train=${train_state} step=${steps}"; exit 0;;
   145	  esac
   146	
   147	  if [ $(( tick % STEP_POLL_EVERY )) -eq 0 ]; then
   148	    steps="$(max_step)"
   149	    echo "$(date -u +%H:%M:%S) t=$(( now - start ))s train=${train_state:-none} step=${steps}"
   150	    # A restored arm resumes at the checkpoint's step, so progress is counted from the first step
   151	    # this watch actually observed rather than from zero.
   152	    if [ "${first_step:--1}" -lt 0 ] && [ "${steps:--1}" -ge 0 ] 2>/dev/null; then
   153	      if [ "$steps" -lt "$EXPECTED_MIN_FIRST_STEP" ]; then
   154	        # Restore silently fell back to scratch (or the wrong checkpoint): the run is logging
   155	        # steps below the checkpoint step. Nothing it measures is valid; kill it immediately.
   156	        cancel_arm "first logged step ${steps} < expected ${EXPECTED_MIN_FIRST_STEP} (restore fell back)"
   157	        echo "WATCHDOG wrong-first-step step=${steps} expected>=${EXPECTED_MIN_FIRST_STEP}"
   158	        exit 1
   159	      fi
   160	      first_step="$steps"
   161	      deadline=$(( now + STEP_BUDGET ))
   162	      echo "$(date -u +%H:%M:%S) first observed step=${first_step}, step budget ${STEP_BUDGET}s starts now"
   163	    fi
   164	    if [ "${first_step:--1}" -ge 0 ] 2>/dev/null && [ $(( steps - first_step )) -ge "$SCORE_MAX_STEP" ]; then
   165	      cancel_arm "scored through step ${steps} (${SCORE_MAX_STEP} past first)"
   166	      echo "WATCHDOG scored step=${steps} first=${first_step}"
   167	      exit 0
   168	    fi
   169	  fi
   170	  tick=$(( tick + 1 ))
   171	  sleep "$POLL"
   172	done

The first concrete risk is already visible: the experiment’s engagement rule equates a scheduler budget with allocator high-water usage. I’m tracing both independently before deciding severity, and I’m also checking the pinned XLA parser path for duplicate-flag semantics rather than assuming shell-style “last wins.”

   188	
   189	def take_master_as_params(state: "GrugTrainState") -> "GrugTrainState":
   190	    """Move a master restored through ``template_for_candidate_layout`` into ``params``."""
   191	    if state.master_params is None:
   192	        return state
   193	    return dataclasses.replace(state, params=state.master_params, master_params=None)

   195	
   196	def _apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults(
   197	    *,
   198	    inline_watch_enabled: bool,
   199	    moe_implementation: MoeImplementation | None,
   200	    remat_mode: RematMode,
   201	    processes_per_task: int = 1,
   202	) -> None:
   203	    env_defaults = dict(HERO_EP_RUNTIME_ENV)
   204	    if processes_per_task > 1:
   205	        # With one process per GPU, the per-process CUPTI sessions collide with each
   206	        # other and with CoreWeave's DCGM, so PGLE cannot profile and its recompile
   207	        # machinery only adds failure modes. Default it off; an explicit env wins.
   208	        env_defaults["JAX_ENABLE_PGLE"] = "false"
   209	    for name, value in env_defaults.items():
   210	        os.environ.setdefault(name, value)
   211	    xla_flags = os.environ.get("XLA_FLAGS", "").split()
   212	    ragged = moe_implementation == RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION
   213	    if ragged:
   214	        overlap_limit = RAGGED_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   215	    elif inline_watch_enabled:
   216	        overlap_limit = INLINE_WATCH_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   217	    else:
   218	        overlap_limit = DEFAULT_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   219	    # The scheduler's longer buffer live ranges fit on the ragged transport only once the layer
   220	    # carry leaves HBM. Without that offload its first-step NCCL allocations fail.
   221	    latency_hiding = not ragged or remat_mode == "offload_carry"
   222	    flag_defaults = (
   223	        f"{XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG}={overlap_limit}",
   224	        f"--xla_gpu_enable_latency_hiding_scheduler={'true' if latency_hiding else 'false'}",
   225	        # The scheduler sizes the single `jit_train_step` temp arena against this percentage of
   226	        # its memory budget, roughly `133.6 GiB x percentage`. The pool holds 138.2 GiB and
   227	        # persistent state occupies 18.1 GiB of it, so an arena above 120.2 GiB cannot be served
   228	        # from pool free space and forces a fresh mapping against the ~17 GiB of physical memory
   229	        # outside the pool. The default 95 asks for 125.7 GiB and fails that way. 85 sizes the
   230	        # arena at 113.6 GiB, leaving enough slack for per-node variation in fragmentation. A
   231	        # lower percentage costs throughput, because a smaller arena makes `HloRematerialization`
   232	        # recompute more of the step.
   233	        "--xla_gpu_memory_limit_slop_factor=85",
   234	        XLA_DISABLE_GPU_COMMAND_BUFFER_FLAG,
   235	    )
   236	    explicit_names = {flag.partition("=")[0] for flag in xla_flags}
   237	    xla_flags.extend(flag for flag in flag_defaults if flag.partition("=")[0] not in explicit_names)
   238	    if remat_mode == "offload_carry":
   239	        # Not overridable, unlike the defaults above, including the scheduler flag it pairs with.
   240	        # Both flags can be inherited from another recipe, but only this one fails silently: a
   241	        # wrong overlap limit corrupts training, while an inherited scheduler setting either
   242	        # aborts in NCCL's first-step allocation and names itself, or runs the slower posture.
   243	        xla_flags = [f for f in xla_flags if f.partition("=")[0] != XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG]
   244	        xla_flags.append(f"{XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG}={OFFLOAD_CARRY_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT}")
   245	    if ragged:
   246	        # Unlike the defaults above, these are not overridable. Selecting the host-launched
   247	        # one-shot kernel needs both flags cleared together plus a splits-per-peer count this
   248	        # branch no longer carries, so honoring a partial override would run a configuration
   249	        # nothing here measures. Drop any conflicting entry rather than relying on which
   250	        # occurrence XLA's parser keeps.
   251	        xla_flags = [f for f in xla_flags if f.partition("=")[0] not in _RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAG_NAMES]
   252	        xla_flags.extend(RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAGS)
   596	
   597	    return flops_per_example, flops_summary
   598	
   599	
   600	def log_device_memory(step_info) -> None:
   601	    """Log this process's local-device HBM peak, live bytes, and allocator limit in GiB.
   602	
   603	    The EP hero had no peak-HBM telemetry, which makes a whole class of result unreadable: XLA's
   604	    ``HloRematerialization`` engages only when peak crosses the allocator limit, so a config change
   605	    that moves peak across that boundary produces an MFU step change that has nothing to do with the
   606	    change itself. Issue #8054 traced its own +9.08% headline to exactly this -- 3.69 GiB of peak
   607	    took it under the limit and switched remat off -- and the win fell to +3.31% once one process
   608	    per GPU put 8.79 GiB back. Any ablation that moves activation memory needs this logged, or its
   609	    rungs cannot be told apart from allocator-limit crossings.
   610	
   611	    Ported from ``experiments/grug/moe_hero_fsdp/train.py``.
   612	    """
   613	    stats = jax.local_devices()[0].memory_stats()
   614	    levanter.tracker.log(
   615	        {
   616	            "memory/peak_gib": stats["peak_bytes_in_use"] / 1024**3,
   617	            "memory/in_use_gib": stats["bytes_in_use"] / 1024**3,
   618	            "memory/limit_gib": stats["bytes_limit"] / 1024**3,
   619	        },
   620	        step=step_info.step,
   621	    )

0.11.1 0.11.1 /home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/jaxlib/__init__.py /home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/jaxlib/_jax.so
def get_compile_options(
    num_replicas: int,
    num_partitions: int,
    device_assignment=None,
    env_options_overrides: dict[str, str] | None = None,
    fdo_profile: bytes | None = None,
    detailed_logging: bool = True,
    backend: xc.Client | None = None,
) -> xc.CompileOptions:
  """Returns the compile options to use, as derived from flag values.

  Args:
    num_replicas: Number of replicas for which to compile.
    num_partitions: Number of partitions for which to compile.
    device_assignment: Optional ndarray of jax devices indicating the assignment
      of logical replicas to physical devices (default inherited from
      xla_client.CompileOptions). Must be consistent with `num_replicas` and
      `num_partitions`.
    env_options_overrides: dict of additional options parsed by the compiler
    fdo_profile: Optional profile for feedback-directed optimization passed to
      XLA.
    detailed_logging: Is this an "interesting" computation about which XLA would
      be wise to log compilation information?
    backend: the client, if available.
  """
  compile_options = xc.CompileOptions()
  compile_options.num_replicas = num_replicas
  compile_options.num_partitions = num_partitions
  build_options = compile_options.executable_build_options
  build_options.use_spmd_partitioning = True
  build_options.use_shardy_partitioner = config.use_shardy_partitioner.value
  if fdo_profile is not None:
    build_options.fdo_profile = fdo_profile
  if device_assignment is not None:
    logger.debug(
        'get_compile_options: num_replicas=%s num_partitions=%s device_assignment=%s',
        num_replicas, num_partitions, device_assignment)
    device_assignment = np.array(device_assignment)

    # Allow 1D device assignment if num_partitions is 1.
    if (device_assignment.ndim == 1) and (num_partitions == 1):
      device_assignment = device_assignment[:, None]

    if num_replicas != device_assignment.shape[0]:
      msg = 'device_assignment does not match num_replicas: {} vs {}.'
      raise ValueError(msg.format(device_assignment, num_replicas))

    if num_partitions != device_assignment.shape[1]:
      msg = 'device_assignment does not match num_partitions: {} vs {}.'
      raise ValueError(msg.format(device_assignment, num_partitions))

    if device_assignment.dtype == object:
      device_assignment = np.vectorize(lambda d: d.id, otypes=[int])(
          device_assignment)
    device_assignment = xc.DeviceAssignment.create(device_assignment)
    assert device_assignment.replica_count() == num_replicas
    assert device_assignment.computation_count() == num_partitions
    compile_options.device_assignment = device_assignment

  build_options.optimization_level = CompilerEffortLevel(
      config.optimization_level.value
  ).value
  build_options.memory_fitting_level = CompilerEffortLevel(
      config.memory_fitting_level.value
  ).value

  if env_options_overrides is not None:
    # Some overrides are passed directly on build_options.
    overrides_on_build_options = ["optimization_level", "memory_fitting_level"]

    env_options_overrides = dict(env_options_overrides)
    for name in overrides_on_build_options:
      if name in env_options_overrides:
        setattr(
            build_options,
            name,
            CompilerEffortLevel(env_options_overrides.pop(name)).value,
        )
    compile_options.env_option_overrides = list(env_options_overrides.items())

  debug_options = compile_options.executable_build_options.debug_options
  if lib.cuda_path is not None:
    debug_options.xla_gpu_cuda_data_dir = lib.cuda_path

  if _DISABLE_MOST_OPTIMIZATIONS.value:
    debug_options.xla_backend_optimization_level = 0
    debug_options.xla_llvm_disable_expensive_passes = True
    debug_options.xla_test_all_input_layouts = False

  if not config.enable_remat_opt_pass.value:
    debug_options.xla_disable_hlo_passes = _add_disabled_hlo_pass(
        debug_options.xla_disable_hlo_passes, "rematerialization")

  # XLA-AutoFDO profile version: precedence order is:
  # 1. Whatever --jax_xla_profile_version is set to.
  # 2. If --jax_xla_profile_version is not set (i.e., 0), call the function
  #    set in get_latest_profile_version and use the return value if non-zero.
  #    If the function returns 0, set -1; this is an error.
  # -1 indicates that no attempt should be made to retrieve the latest profile
  # later on.
  jax_xla_profile_version = config.jax_xla_profile_version.value
  if jax_xla_profile_version > 0:
    compile_options.profile_version = jax_xla_profile_version
    logger.debug("get_compile_options XLA-AutoFDO profile: " +
                 "using JAX XLA profile version %d from flag",
                 jax_xla_profile_version)
  else:
    compile_options.profile_version = _NO_PROFILE_DONT_RETRIEVE
    if backend is None:
      logger.info("get_compile_options: no backend supplied; "
                   "disabling XLA-AutoFDO profile")
    else:
      fdo_profile_version = get_latest_profile_version(backend)
      if fdo_profile_version != 0:
        compile_options.profile_version = fdo_profile_version
        logger.debug("get_compile_options XLA-AutoFDO profile: " +
                     "using XLA-AutoFDO profile version %d",
                     fdo_profile_version)
      else:
        logger.error("get_compile_options XLA-AutoFDO profile: " +
                     "XLA-AutoFDO profile version is 0; this should not happen")

  debug_options.xla_detailed_logging = detailed_logging

  # If persistent cache is enabled, also enable additional XLA caching features.
  if compilation_cache.is_persistent_cache_enabled():
    # compilation_cache_dir can't be None here, but the type checker is a bit
    # strict.
    path = pathlib.Path(config.compilation_cache_dir.value or "")
    enabled_flags = config.persistent_cache_enable_xla_caches.value or ""

    if enabled_flags == "all" or "xla_gpu_kernel_cache_file" in enabled_flags:
      kernel_cache_path = path / "xla_gpu_kernel_cache_file"
      debug_options.xla_gpu_kernel_cache_file = str(kernel_cache_path)
      logger.debug("Enabling XLA kernel cache at '%s'", kernel_cache_path)

    if enabled_flags == "all" or "xla_gpu_per_fusion_autotune_cache_dir" in enabled_flags:
      autotune_cache_path = path / "xla_gpu_per_fusion_autotune_cache_dir"
      debug_options.xla_gpu_per_fusion_autotune_cache_dir = str(autotune_cache_path)
      logger.debug("Enabling XLA autotuning cache at '%s'", autotune_cache_path)

      # Set caching mode so that only process 0 can write to the cache.
      if distributed.global_state.process_id == 0:
        debug_options.xla_gpu_experimental_autotune_cache_mode = xc.AutotuneCacheMode.UPDATE
      else:
        debug_options.xla_gpu_experimental_autotune_cache_mode = xc.AutotuneCacheMode.READ

  return compile_options

   255	def test_run_grug_defaults_pgle_off_for_per_gpu_processes(monkeypatch):
   256	    # Per-GPU processes cannot profile: the per-process CUPTI sessions collide with
   257	    # each other and with the cluster's DCGM, and auto-PGLE's recompile path has
   258	    # wedged per-node gangs (#7344). Per-GPU runs therefore default PGLE off, while
   259	    # an explicit env setting still wins.
   260	    for name in train.HERO_EP_RUNTIME_ENV:
   261	        monkeypatch.delenv(name, raising=False)
   262	    monkeypatch.delenv("XLA_FLAGS", raising=False)
   263	    config = _runtime_env_config(processes_per_task=4)
   264	
   265	    with patch.object(train, "dispatch_grug_training_run"):
   266	        train.run_grug(config)
   267	
   268	    assert os.environ["JAX_ENABLE_PGLE"] == "false"
   269

   271	    with patch.object(train, "dispatch_grug_training_run"):
   272	        train.run_grug(config)

   437	def test_the_carry_offload_overrides_an_inherited_collective_overlap_limit(monkeypatch):
   438	    """The offload, the scheduler, and an overlap limit above 1 corrupt training together (#8317).
   439	
   440	    An overlap limit inherited from another recipe's environment is the way a run reaches that
   441	    combination without anyone choosing it, and the corruption is silent, so the offload takes the
   442	    flag away from the caller instead of defaulting it.
   443	    """
   444	    inherited = f"{train.XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG}={train.DEFAULT_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT}"
   445	    monkeypatch.setenv("XLA_FLAGS", inherited)
   446	    config = _runtime_env_config(moe_implementation=train.RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION, remat_mode="offload_carry")
   447	
   448	    with patch.object(train, "dispatch_grug_training_run"):
   449	        train.run_grug(config)
   450	
   451	    flags = os.environ["XLA_FLAGS"].split()
   452	    assert inherited not in flags
   453	    assert f"{train.XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG}=1" in flags

     1	# Copyright The Marin Authors

     4	"""Bounded GB200 diagnostics for the production EP64 MoE hero recipe."""
     5

    57	)
