   256	def verify_ragged_pjrt() -> None:
   257	    """Raise unless this process runs Marin's patched GPU PJRT plugin."""
   258	    try:
   259	        installed = importlib.metadata.version(PJRT_DISTRIBUTION)
   260	    except importlib.metadata.PackageNotFoundError as missing:
   261	        raise RuntimeError(
   262	            f"{PJRT_DISTRIBUTION} is not installed, so this process has no GPU PJRT plugin at all."
   263	        ) from missing
   264	    expected_prefix = f"{jax.__version__}+marin."
   265	    if not installed.startswith(expected_prefix):
   266	        raise RuntimeError(
   267	            f"{RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION} needs Marin's patched {PJRT_DISTRIBUTION} "
   268	            f"({expected_prefix}*), found {installed}. The patched wheel is aarch64-only and the "
   269	            "expert MLP is SM100-specialized; run the ragged transport on GB200."
   270	        )
   271	
   272	
   273	@dataclass(frozen=True)
   274	class GrugTrainerConfig:
   275	    """Runtime knobs for grug training."""
   276	
   277	    trainer: TrainerConfig = field(default_factory=lambda: TrainerConfig(use_explicit_mesh_axes=True))
   278	    data_seed: int | None = None
   279	    log_every: int = 1
   280	    ema_beta: float | None = None  # EMA coefficient for eval/checkpoint model; None disables EMA.
   281	    z_loss_weight: float = 1e-4  # Weight on final-logit logsumexp z-loss stabilization term.
   282	    # Keep disabled except on model sizes where Grace-Blackwell host offload has been measured.
   283	    # The d6144 EP64 runs used it; d5120 required a 135 GiB pinned-host arena and regressed.
   284	    offload_opt_state: bool = False
   285	    master_param_mode: MasterParamMode = MasterParamMode.DEVICE
   286	    training_data_mode: TrainingDataMode = TrainingDataMode.MIXTURE
   287	    # Inline watch computes statistics on every step and uses the watch interval only for logging.
   288	    # This keeps one training executable resident. A diagnostic watch repeats forward and backward
   289	    # in a separate executable, which costs compute but shortens gradient liveness.
   290	    watch_mode: WatchMode = WatchMode.INLINE
   291	    # A short throughput gate leaves this off. A compute-optimal run needs it: the loop already
   292	    # restores from the latest committed checkpoint, so without a writer an interrupted run
   293	    # restarts at step 0.
   294	    save_checkpoints: bool = False
   295	
   296	    # Grug builds its own compact (replica_dcn, data, expert, model) mesh instead of using
   297	    # the Trainer's logical axis mapping; `data` absorbs whatever these two leave free.
   298	    # Defaults reproduce the historical layout: no expert parallelism and full replication
   299	    # across slices (replica_axis_size=None -> jax.process_count()), i.e. parameters
   300	    # replicated per slice and sharded only over the intra-slice `data` axis. For a model
   301	    # too large to replicate within one slice, set replica_axis_size=1 (FSDP across every
   302	    # slice) and expert_axis_size>1 (expert parallelism over the intra-slice devices).
   303	    expert_axis_size: int = 1
   304	    replica_axis_size: int | None = None
   305	    sharding_dump_path: str | None = None
   306	
   307	
   308	@dataclass(frozen=True)
   309	class GrugEvalConfig:
   310	    """Perplexity eval settings for grug training."""
   311	
   312	    eval_batch_size: int = 512
   313	    steps_per_eval: int | None = 1000
   314	    max_eval_batches: int | None = None
   315	    prefix: str = "eval"
   316	    eval_current: bool = True
   317	    eval_ema: bool = True
   318	    compute_bpb: bool = True
   319	    # For expert-parallel runs, also evaluate under the dropless local backend on an
   320	    # expert-collapsed mesh, logging a separate `eval_dropless` macro loss alongside the
   321	    # as-trained (with-drop) eval. No-op when the mesh has no expert parallelism.
   322	    dropless_eval: bool = False
   323	    # Local MoE kernel used after collapsing the expert axis. ``sonic`` is the Hopper Triton path;
   324	    # ``sonic_cute`` is the Blackwell QuACK/CUTLASS path.
   325	    dropless_eval_moe_implementation: MoeImplementation = DEFAULT_DROPLESS_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION
   326	    # Run the evals once after the first optimization step, for a baseline at the start of the loss
   327	    # curve. The periodic cadence first fires at `steps_per_eval`, thus it leaves that start bare.
   328	    eval_at_first_step: bool = False
   329	
   330	
   331	@dataclass(frozen=True)
   332	class GrugRunConfig:
   333	    """Top-level config for grug training."""
   334	
   335	    model: GrugModelConfig
   336	    data: LmDataConfig
   337	    resources: ResourceConfig
   338	    tensorstore_cache_bytes: int | None = None
   339	    optimizer: OptimizerConfig = field(default_factory=AdamConfig)
   340	    trainer: GrugTrainerConfig = field(default_factory=GrugTrainerConfig)
   341	    eval: GrugEvalConfig | None = field(default_factory=GrugEvalConfig)
   342	    # Stop after this many steps while `trainer.num_train_steps` still sizes the learning-rate
   343	    # schedule. Warmup and decay are fractions of `num_train_steps`, so training the head of a
   344	    # long schedule requires the two to differ. None runs the whole schedule.
   345	    stop_after_steps: int | None = None
   346	    # GPU processes per task: > 1 runs one JAX process per GPU (multi-controller)
   347	    # via the iris.hooks.multigpu_main supervisor instead of one process per node.
   348	    processes_per_task: int = 1
   349	    # Retry budgets for the training job. The two are separate gates and the job fails when either
   350	    # one trips, thus raise them together. The defaults make a failure terminal, which is what a run
   260	            batch_size=batch_size,
   261	            max_seq_len=model.max_seq_len,
   262	            experiment_flops=experiment_flops,
   263	            validation=validation,
   264	        )
   265	        return GrugRunConfig(
   266	            model=model,
   267	            data=data,
   268	            resources=ctx.runtime_arg("train_resources"),
   269	            tensorstore_cache_bytes=HERO_TENSORSTORE_CACHE_BYTES,
   270	            optimizer=optimizer,
   271	            trainer=dataclasses.replace(grug_trainer, trainer=trainer),
   272	            # Off by default so a throughput run stays a throughput run. Turn it on to make a
   273	            # run scoreable: comparing configs needs held-out loss, not train loss.
   274	            eval=(
   275	                GrugEvalConfig(
   276	                    steps_per_eval=eval_every,
   277	                    eval_batch_size=HERO_EP_EXPERT_AXIS_SIZE * dp_racks,
   278	                    eval_ema=False,
   279	                    compute_bpb=True,
   280	                    dropless_eval=True,
   281	                )
   282	                if eval_every > 0
   283	                else None
   284	            ),
   285	            stop_after_steps=num_steps,
   286	            processes_per_task=processes_per_task,
   287	        )
   288	
   289	    return ArtifactStep(
   290	        name=user_namespaced_name(name, version),
   291	        version=version,
   292	        artifact_type=HeroThroughputResult,
   293	        run=run_grug,
   294	        build_config=build_config,
   295	        deps=(HARRIER_MIX_2026_08_18_STORE, *validation),
   296	        runtime_args={"train_resources": train_resources},
   297	    )
   298	
   299	
   300	@click.command()
   301	@click.option("--run-id", required=True, help="Run identifier for artifact and W&B names.")
   302	@click.option(
   303	    "--dp-racks",
   304	    type=click.IntRange(min=1),

   308	)

     4	import abc
     5	import dataclasses
     6	import logging
     7	import typing
     8	from typing import Any, List, Optional
     9	
    10	import draccus
    11	import jax
    12	
    13	
    14	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

    16	
    17	class Tracker(abc.ABC):
    18	    """
    19	    A tracker is responsible for logging metrics, hyperparameters, and artifacts.
    20	    Meant to be used with the [levanter.tracker.current_tracker][] context manager, but can also be used directly.
    21	
    22	    The name is borrowed from HF Accelerate.
    23	
    24	    Examples:
    25	        >>> from levanter.tracker import current_tracker, log
    26	        >>> from levanter.tracker.wandb import WandbTracker
    27	        >>> with current_tracker(WandbTracker()):
    28	        ...     log({"foo": 1}, step=0)
    29	    """
    30	
    31	    name: str
    32	
    33	    def _prepare_log(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, Any]) -> Any:
    34	        """Convert a ``log`` payload to host data, on the caller thread.
    35	
    36	        Both ``log`` and :class:`~levanter.tracker.background.BackgroundTracker`
    37	        call this. The default pulls ``jax.Array`` leaves to host with
    38	        ``jax.device_get``; subclasses override to also fold in backend-specific
    39	        conversion. Must be idempotent — the background worker re-invokes ``log``
    40	        on the result.
    41	        """
    42	        return jax.device_get(metrics)
    43	
    44	    def _prepare_summary(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, Any]) -> Any:
    45	        """Producer-thread counterpart to :meth:`_prepare_log` for ``log_summary``."""
    46	        return jax.device_get(metrics)
    47	
    48	    def _prepare_hyperparameters(self, hparams: typing.Mapping[str, Any]) -> Any:
    49	        """Producer-thread counterpart to :meth:`_prepare_log` for ``log_hyperparameters``."""
    50	        return jax.device_get(hparams)
    51	
    52	    @abc.abstractmethod
    53	    def log_hyperparameters(self, hparams: dict[str, Any]):
    54	        pass
    55	
    56	    @abc.abstractmethod
    57	    def log(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, typing.Any], *, step: Optional[int], commit: Optional[bool] = None):
    58	        """
    59	        Log metrics to the tracker. Step is always required.
    60	
    61	        Args:
    62	            metrics: Metrics to log
    63	            step: Step to log at
    64	            commit: Whether to commit the metrics. If None, uses the default for the tracker.
    65	        """
    66	        pass
    67	
    68	    @abc.abstractmethod
    69	    def log_summary(self, metrics: dict[str, Any]):
    70	        pass
    71	
    72	    @abc.abstractmethod
    73	    def log_artifact(self, artifact_path, *, name: Optional[str] = None, type: Optional[str] = None):
    74	        pass
    75	
    76	    def log_html(self, key: str, html_path, *, step: Optional[int], commit: Optional[bool] = None):
    77	        pass
    78	
    79	    def capture_stall_diagnostics(self) -> None:
    80	        """Capture tracker-specific diagnostics before watchdog termination."""
    81	        pass
    82	
    83	    @abc.abstractmethod
    84	    def finish(self):
    85	        """
    86	        Finish the tracker. This is called when the tracker is no longer needed. This can, e.g.,
    87	        force a commit of all metrics.
    88	        """
    89	        pass
    90	
    91	    def __enter__(self):
    92	        # Deferred: tracker_fns eagerly imports the wandb/trackio backends, which import
    93	        # `background`, which imports this module -> a genuine cycle. __enter__ only needs
    94	        # the global-tracker accessor at call time, so import it lazily here.
    95	        import levanter.tracker.tracker_fns as tracker_fns  # noqa: PLC0415
    96	
    97	        if hasattr(self, "_tracker_cm"):
    98	            raise RuntimeError("This tracker is already set as the global tracker")
    99	        setattr(self, "_tracker_cm", tracker_fns.current_tracker(self))
   100	        self._tracker_cm.__enter__()
   101	
   102	    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc_val, exc_tb):
   103	        if not hasattr(self, "_tracker_cm"):
   104	            raise RuntimeError("This tracker is not set as the global tracker")
   105	        self._tracker_cm.__exit__(exc_type, exc_val, exc_tb)
   106	        delattr(self, "_tracker_cm")
   107	
   108	
   109	class CompositeTracker(Tracker):
   110	    """A tracker that fans calls out to a list of trackers.
   111	
   112	    Exceptions from any single member tracker are caught and logged so that one
   113	    failing backend (e.g. W&B losing connectivity) doesn't take down the others.
   114	    Wrap members in :class:`~levanter.tracker.BackgroundTracker` if you also
   115	    want isolation from latency.
   116	    """
   117	
   118	    def __init__(self, loggers: List[Tracker]):
   119	        self.loggers = loggers
   120	
   121	    def _for_each(self, op: str, *args, **kwargs) -> None:
   122	        for tracker in self.loggers:
   123	            try:
   124	                getattr(tracker, op)(*args, **kwargs)
   125	            except Exception:
   126	                logger.exception(
   127	                    "Tracker '%s' raised during %s; continuing with remaining trackers.",
   128	                    getattr(tracker, "name", type(tracker).__name__),
   129	                    op,
   130	                )
   131	
   132	    def log_hyperparameters(self, hparams: dict[str, Any]):
   133	        self._for_each("log_hyperparameters", hparams)
   134	
   135	    def log(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, Any], *, step, commit=None):
   136	        self._for_each("log", metrics, step=step, commit=commit)
   137	
   138	    def log_summary(self, metrics: dict[str, Any]):
   139	        self._for_each("log_summary", metrics)
   140	
   141	    def log_artifact(self, artifact_path, *, name: Optional[str] = None, type: Optional[str] = None):
   142	        self._for_each("log_artifact", artifact_path, name=name, type=type)
   143	
   144	    def log_html(self, key: str, html_path, *, step: Optional[int], commit: Optional[bool] = None):
   145	        self._for_each("log_html", key, html_path, step=step, commit=commit)
   146	
   147	    def capture_stall_diagnostics(self) -> None:
   148	        self._for_each("capture_stall_diagnostics")
   149	
   150	    def finish(self):
   151	        # finish() exceptions are logged and swallowed too; a tracker failing to
   152	        # flush at the very end of a run shouldn't crash the trainer's shutdown.
   153	        self._for_each("finish")
   154	
   155	
   156	class TrackerConfig(draccus.PluginRegistry, abc.ABC):
   157	    discover_packages_path = "levanter.tracker"
   158	
   159	    @abc.abstractmethod
   160	    def init(self, run_id: Optional[str]) -> Tracker:
   161	        raise NotImplementedError
   162	
   163	    @classmethod
   164	    def default_choice_name(cls) -> Optional[str]:
   165	        return "wandb"
   166	
   167	
   168	class NoopTracker(Tracker):
   169	    name: str = "noop"
   170	
   171	    def log_hyperparameters(self, hparams: dict[str, Any]):
   172	        pass
   173	
   174	    def log(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, Any], *, step, commit: Optional[bool] = None):
   175	        pass
   176	
   177	    def log_summary(self, metrics: dict[str, Any]):
   178	        pass
   179	
   180	    def log_artifact(self, artifact_path, *, name: Optional[str] = None, type: Optional[str] = None):
   181	        pass
   182	
   183	    def finish(self):
   184	        pass
   185	
   186	
   187	@TrackerConfig.register_subclass("noop")
   188	@dataclasses.dataclass
   189	class NoopConfig(TrackerConfig):
   190	    def init(self, run_id: Optional[str]) -> Tracker:
   191	        return NoopTracker()
   192	
   193	
   194	class DictTracker(Tracker):
   195	    """
   196	    A tracker that logs to a dictionary. We mostly use this to smuggle things outside of jit
   197	    """
   198	
   199	    def __init__(self):
   200	        self.metrics: dict[str, Any] = {}
   201	
   202	    def log_hyperparameters(self, hparams: dict[str, Any]):
   203	        self.metrics["hparams"] = hparams
   204	
   205	    def log(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, Any], *, step: Optional[int], commit: Optional[bool] = None):
   206	        if step is not None:
   207	            self.metrics[f"step_{step}"] = metrics
   208	        else:
   209	            self.metrics.update(metrics)
   210	
   100	                runx = wandb.init()
   101	                if runx is None:
   102	                    raise RuntimeError("Wandb run is not initialized.")
   103	                self.run = runx
   104	            else:
   105	                self.run = wandb.run
   106	        else:
   107	            self.run = run
   108	
   109	        self._last_warning_step = -500
   110	        self._replicate_path = replicate_path
   111	        self._suppress_logging = suppress_logging
   112	        self._minimum_log_step = minimum_log_step
   113	
   114	    # The prepare hooks do the full wandb-side conversion (SummaryStats expansion,
   115	    # wandb.Histogram construction) so the background worker only uploads. They are
   116	    # idempotent, so re-running them on an already-prepared payload is a no-op.
   117	
   118	    def _prepare_log(self, metrics):
   119	        return _convert_metrics_to_wandb_loggable(metrics)
   120	
   121	    def _prepare_summary(self, metrics):
   122	        return _convert_value_to_loggable_rec(metrics)
   123	
   124	    def _prepare_hyperparameters(self, hparams):
   125	        return _convert_value_to_loggable_rec(hparams)
   126	
   127	    def log_hyperparameters(self, hparams: dict[str, Any]):
   128	        if self._suppress_logging:
   129	            return
   130	        self.run.config.update(self._prepare_hyperparameters(hparams), allow_val_change=True)
   131	
   132	    def log(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, Any], *, step, commit=None):
   133	        if step is None and not commit:
   134	            step = self.run.step
   135	
   136	        if step < self._minimum_log_step:
   137	            if step - self._last_warning_step > 500:
   138	                logger.warning(
   139	                    f"Step {step} is less than the current step {self._minimum_log_step}. "
   140	                    "Cowardly refusing to log metrics."
   141	                )
   142	                self._last_warning_step = step
   143	            return
   144	
   145	        step = int(step)
   146	
   147	        to_log = self._prepare_log(metrics)
   148	
   149	        if self._suppress_logging:
   150	            return
   151	
   152	        if step < self.run.step:
   153	            if step - self._last_warning_step > 500:
   154	                logger.warning(
   155	                    f"Step {step} is less than the current step {self.run.step}. Cowardly refusing to log metrics."
   156	                )
   157	                self._last_warning_step = step
   158	            return
   159	
   160	        self.run.log(to_log, step=step, commit=commit)
   161	
   162	    def log_summary(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, Any]):
   163	        if self._suppress_logging:
   164	            return
   165	        self.run.summary.update(self._prepare_summary(metrics))
   166	
   167	    def log_artifact(self, artifact_path, *, name: Optional[str] = None, type: Optional[str] = None):
   168	        if self._suppress_logging:
   169	            return
   170	        artifact_name = name if name is not None else _default_wandb_artifact_name(artifact_path)
   171	        self.run.log_artifact(
   172	            artifact_path,
   173	            name=_truncate_wandb_artifact_name(artifact_name),
   174	            type=type,
   175	        )
   176	
   177	    def log_html(self, key: str, html_path, *, step: Optional[int], commit: Optional[bool] = None):
   178	        if step is None and not commit:
   179	            step = self.run.step
   180	        if step is not None and step < self.run.step:
   181	            if step - self._last_warning_step > 500:
   182	                logger.warning(
   183	                    f"Step {step} is less than the current step {self.run.step}. Cowardly refusing to log HTML."
   184	                )
   185	                self._last_warning_step = step
   186	            return
   187	
   188	        wandb_step = None if step is None else int(step)
   189	        self.run.log({key: wandb.Html(str(html_path))}, step=wandb_step, commit=commit)
   190	
   191	    def finish(self):
   192	        if self._suppress_logging:
   193	            return
   194	
   195	        # Write the replicate file before touching wandb: run.finish() can wedge
   196	        # past the background finish timeout, and by the time it raises the
   197	        # process is tearing down and fsspec can no longer schedule writes.
   198	        # This write is best-effort insurance — its failure must not keep
   199	        # run.finish() from running — and its summary may miss the final
   200	        # commit, so a successful finish rewrites the file with the flushed
   201	        # values below.
   202	        try:
   203	            self._write_replicate_file()
   204	        except Exception:
   205	            logger.exception("Pre-finish replicate write failed; retrying after wandb finish.")
   206	
   207	        logger.info("Finishing wandb run...")
   208	        self.run.finish()
   209	        self._write_replicate_file()
   210

codex
Disposition: block H6 as currently specified. The `90` flag reaches the train process correctly, but the engagement check can both reject a live lever and validate an unrelated peak change.

### Ranked findings

1. **Blocker — `memory/peak_gib must rise` is neither necessary nor sufficient for engagement.**

   [`log_device_memory`](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:600) reports allocator `peak_bytes_in_use`; it does not report the compiler’s scheduler limit, reset the peak before the scored window, or cover every rank. W&B suppresses non-primary processes, so the metric represents process 0 only ([wandb.py:397](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/tracker/wandb.py:397)).

   In pinned XLA, slop computes a limit `(base_limit - I/O) * factor / 100`; it does not allocate an arena of that size ([gpu_hlo_schedule.cc:997–1052](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c0272/xla/service/gpu/gpu_hlo_schedule.cc#L997-L1052)). The limit separately guides LHS scheduling and later HLO rematerialization. LHS can reorder choices once tracked usage exceeds half the limit, so a genuine schedule change can occur without changing the final allocator high-water mark ([latency_hiding_scheduler.cc:1664–1675](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c0272/xla/service/latency_hiding_scheduler.cc#L1664-L1675)). Conversely, rank-0 allocator fragmentation or an earlier allocation can raise the high-water mark without proving a remat change.

   Replace the gate with two checks:

   - Configuration engagement: the train-task log contains exactly one `--xla_gpu_memory_limit_slop_factor=90`.
   - Mechanism engagement: compare compiler evidence. XLA already logs `LatencyHidingScheduler current memory usage ... Current limit` at INFO ([latency_hiding_scheduler.cc:4400–4403](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c0272/xla/service/latency_hiding_scheduler.cc#L4400-L4403)). Also capture HloRematerialization’s limit, rematerialized-instruction count, and before/after compiler peak through VLOG(1), or compare the control/treatment scheduled HLO ([hlo_rematerialization.cc:3108–3109](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c0272/xla/hlo/transforms/simplifiers/hlo_rematerialization.cc#L3108-L3109), [3271–3293](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c0272/xla/hlo/transforms/simplifiers/hlo_rematerialization.cc#L3271-L3293)).

   If the limit changes and either LHS schedule or remat count changes, the lever engaged even when `peak_gib_max` stays flat. Keep peak HBM as supporting safety telemetry.

2. **Major — the env-only treatment is not recorded in `arms.tsv`.**

   The arm spec says `ARM_XLA_FLAGS` is recorded in an extra column ([LOGBOOK.md:104](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/LOGBOOK.md:104)). The ledger instead records only `EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS`; neither the appended row nor the final submission message includes `ARM_XLA_FLAGS` ([arm.sh:97](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/arm.sh:97)). Because H6 has no commit, the ledger makes treatment and control indistinguishable. This creates a direct false-attribution risk even if task logs later contain the effective flag.

3. **Major — the fidelity guard can false-fail because its tolerance is not operationally defined for this comparison.**

   The campaign applies a pointwise 15-step maximum delta but describes the threshold only as a “bf16-noise band,” citing a last-step range from four 24k arms ([DESIGN.md:87](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md:87), [DESIGN.md:102](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md:102)). That does not define a pointwise 30k control-control tolerance. `score.py` also rounds losses to six decimals before comparison material is emitted ([score.py:101](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/score.py:101)).

   Numerical identity is plausible but not guaranteed. This recipe computes in bf16 ([hero_recipe.py:40](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/hero_recipe.py:40)), and XLA permits rematerialization of side-effect-free operations and unconstrained collectives ([hlo_rematerialization.cc:89–116](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c0272/xla/hlo/transforms/simplifiers/hlo_rematerialization.cc#L89-L116)). Any low-bit gradient difference can propagate through the following 14 updates.

   Predeclare the tolerance from the iteration-0 control-control pointwise loss deltas under this exact 30k protocol. A small exceedance should trigger another paired draw or numerical investigation, not automatically kill H6. Non-finite loss, changed drops, or a deviation beyond the empirical control envelope remains a hard failure.

4. **Major — binding is unverified, although the stack does not disable the knob.**

   The forced configuration keeps LHS enabled for `offload_carry` ([train.py:219](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:219)) and separately forces overlap limit 1 ([train.py:238](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:238)). Pinned XLA passes the memory limit and overlap limit as distinct scheduler fields ([gpu_hlo_schedule.cc:1055–1067](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c0272/xla/service/gpu/gpu_hlo_schedule.cc#L1055-L1067)), then passes the same scheduler limit into HloRematerialization ([gpu_compiler.cc:3298–3324](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c0272/xla/service/gpu/gpu_compiler.cc#L3298-L3324)). Therefore overlap-limit 1 does not make slop dead.

   Static code cannot establish that 85 binds this compiled graph. Runtime `116.5 GiB` and compiler `113.6 GiB` use different accounting and cannot answer that question. Control compiler logs must show either memory-pressure-sensitive LHS behavior or nonzero rematerialization before the “less recompute” mechanism is credited.

5. **Moderate — the watchdog preserves validity but cannot classify an OOM for the H6 ladder.**

   A prompt process exit becomes generic `WATCHDOG task-failed` or `WATCHDOG terminal`; the watchdog never reads task logs ([watchdog.sh:137](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/watchdog.sh:137)). An allocator/NCCL failure that wedges before exit becomes a generic compile- or step-budget kill ([watchdog.sh:91](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/watchdog.sh:91)). Since retries default to zero ([train.py:349](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:349)), a prompt OOM will not produce a valid scored arm, but the marker alone cannot distinguish “ladder to 88” from unrelated infrastructure failure or a resubmission case. Require task-log confirmation of BFC/CUDA/NCCL allocation failure before applying the OOM ladder.

### Precedence verification

The proposed treatment does not produce duplicate slop flags:

- [`arm.sh:80`](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/arm.sh:80) injects `ARM_XLA_FLAGS` as `XLA_FLAGS`.
- [`train.py:211–237`](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:211) sees the explicit slop name and omits default `85`.
- The forced-remat and ragged branches remove only overlap/ragged-required names, leaving slop untouched ([train.py:238](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:238)).
- [`dispatch.py:29–38`](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/dispatch.py:29) forwards `XLA_FLAGS` to the fresh train process.

The resulting train-side list contains exactly `slop_factor=90`. If duplicates were introduced elsewhere, pinned XLA parses arguments sequentially and invokes the setter for each occurrence, so the last occurrence wins ([command_line_flags.cc:250–283](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c0272/xla/tsl/util/command_line_flags.cc#L250-L283)).

- [`dispatch.py:29–38`](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/dispatch.py:29) forwards `XLA_FLAGS` to the fresh train process.

The resulting train-side list contains exactly `slop_factor=90`. If duplicates were introduced elsewhere, pinned XLA parses arguments sequentially and invokes the setter for each occurrence, so the last occurrence wins ([command_line_flags.cc:250–283](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c0272/xla/tsl/util/command_line_flags.cc#L250-L283)).
